B.3.
Properties of
\mathsf{GCIC}

Conservativity is an equivalence, so to prove it we break it down into two implications. We now state and prove those in an open context and for the three different judgments.

Theorem 3 (
\mathsf{GCIC}
is weaker than
\mathsf{CIC}
—Open context).

Let
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
be a static term and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
an erasable context. Then

•

if
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{CIC}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}T
then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
for some erasable
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})=T
;

•

if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
is an erasable term of
\mathsf{CastCIC}
, and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{CIC}}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
for some erasable
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
such that
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
;

•

if
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{CIC}}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}T
then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
for some erasable
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})=T
.

Once again, the proof is by mutual induction, on the typing derivation of
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
in
\mathsf{CIC}
.

All inference rules are direct: one needs to combine the induction hypothesis together, using the substitution property of precision and the fact that erasure commutes with substitution to handle the cases of substitution in the inferred types.

Let us consider the case of
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
next. We are given
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
erasable, and suppose
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{CIC}}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleright}T
and
T\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B
. By induction hypothesis there exists
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
erasable such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
,
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})=T
. Because
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
is erasable, it is less precise than
T
. By
Corollary

17
, it must reduce to either
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square}
or a product type. The first case is impossible because
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
does not contain any
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}
as it is erasable. Thus there are some
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
. Since also
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}T
, by the same reasoning there are also some
A^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}
such that
T\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime\prime}.B^{\prime\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime\prime}.B^{\prime\prime}
. Now because
T
is static, so are
\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B
and
\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime\prime}.B^{\prime\prime}
, and because of the comparisons with
\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
we must have
\varepsilon(\Gamma)\vdash\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime\prime}.B^{\prime\prime}
. Since both are static, this means they must be
\alpha
-equal, since no non-diagonal rule can be used on static terms. Hence,
\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B=\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime\prime}.B^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime})
, implying that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
is erasable.
Thus,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\Pi}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
, both
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
are erasable, and moreover
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime})=\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B
, which is what had to be proven.

The other constrained inference rules being very similar, let us turn to
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
. We are given
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
erasable, and suppose that
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{CIC}}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleright}S
such that
S\equiv\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
.
By induction hypothesis,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}
with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}
erasable,
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime})=S
. But convertibility implies consistency, so
S\sim\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
.
By monotonicity of consistency, this implies
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}\sim\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
. Thus
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T^{\prime}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t}
. We have
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T^{\prime}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t})=\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t)=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
, so we are left with showing that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T^{\prime}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t}\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
. Using rules
10Structural precision in

CastCIC

and
10Structural precision in

CastCIC

, and knowing already that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}S
, it remains to show that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\leadsto}S
and
\Gamma\vdash S\sqsubseteq_{\leadsto}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
. As
S
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
are convertible, let
U
be a common reduct. Using
Theorem

16
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U^{\prime}
with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash U\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U^{\prime}
. Simulating that reduction again, we get
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}U^{\prime\prime}
with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash U^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U^{\prime}
. As before, this implies
U=U^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U^{\prime})
. Thus, using the reduct
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
that is equiprecise with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}U
, we can conclude
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash S\sqsubseteq_{\leadsto}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\leadsto}S
.
∎

Theorem 4 (
\mathsf{CIC}
is weaker than
\mathsf{GCIC}
—Open context).

Let
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
be a static term and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
an erasable context of
\mathsf{CastCIC}
. Then

•

if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
, then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
are erasable,
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime})=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleright}\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
;

•

if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
is an erasable term of
\mathsf{CastCIC}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
, then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
is erasable,
\varepsilon(t^{\prime})=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
;

•

if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
, then
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
are erasable,
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime})=\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma)\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})
.

The proof is similar to the previous one. Again, the tricky part is to handle reduction steps, and we use equiprecision in the same way to conclude in those.
∎

As a direct corollary of those propositions in an empty context, we get conservativity
Theorem

21
.

Elaboration graduality

Now for the elaboration graduality: again, we state it in an open context for all three typing judgments.

Theorem 5 (Elaboration graduality—Open context).

Let
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}
be a context such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}
, and
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
be two
\mathsf{GCIC}
terms such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
. Then

•

if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
is universe adequate, then there exists
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
;

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}t\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
is universe adequate, then for all
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
there exists
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
;

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}t\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
is universe adequate, then there exists
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{h}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
.

Once again, we use our favorite tool: induction on the typing derivation of
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
.

Inference—Non-diagonal precision.

For inference, we have to make a distinction on the rule used to prove
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}tilde{t}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
: we have to handle specifically the non-diagonal one, where
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
is some
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}
. We start with this, and treat the ones where the rule is diagonal (
i.e.,
when
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
have the same head) next.

We have
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}\scalebox{0.7}{@\lx@text@lbrace i\lx@text@rbrace}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square_{i}}}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square_{i}}
. Correctness of elaboration gives
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
, and by validity
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
, universe adequacy ensuring us that this
i
is the same as the one in
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
. Thus we have
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square_{i}}
by rule
10Structural precision in

CastCIC

, and in turn
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square_{i}}}
by a second use of the same rule, giving us the required conclusions.

Inference—Variable.

Rule
9Type-directed elaboration from

GCIC

to

CastCIC

gives us
(x:\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T)\in\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}
. Because
\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}
, there exists some
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
such that
(x:\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime})\in\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}
, and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
using weakening. Thus,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}x\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
, and of course
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}x
.

Inference—Product.

Premises of rule
9Type-directed elaboration from

GCIC

to

CastCIC

give
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{A}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\square}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1},x:A\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{B}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\square}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{j}
, and the diagonal precision one gives
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{A}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{A}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{B}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{B}^{\prime}
. Applying the induction hypothesis, we get some
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{A}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\square}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
. The inferred type for
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{A}^{\prime}
must be
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
as it is some
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{j}
because of the constrained elaboration, and it is less precise than
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\square_{i}
by the induction hypothesis.
From this, we also deduce that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1},x:A\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2},x:A^{\prime}
. Hence the induction hypothesis can be applied to
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{B}
, giving
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{B}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\square}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{j}
. Combining this with the elaboration for
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\tilde{A}^{\prime}
, we obtain
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\operatorname{\Pi}x:\tilde{A}^{\prime}.\tilde{B}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{s_{\Pi}(i,j)}
. Moreover,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
by combining the precision hypothesis on
A
and
B
, and also
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{s_{\Pi}(i,j)}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{s_{\Pi}(i,j)}
.

Inference—Application.

From rule
9Type-directed elaboration from

GCIC

to

CastCIC

, we have
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\Pi}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{u}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u
, and the diagonal precision gives
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{u}\sqsubseteq^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{u}^{\prime}
. By induction, we have
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\Pi}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}
for some
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma},x:A\mid A^{\prime}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}
. Using the induction hypothesis again with that precision property on
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}
gives
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{u}^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u^{\prime}
with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u^{\prime}
.
Therefore combining those we get
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}~\tilde{u}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}~u^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}[u^{\prime}/x]
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t~u\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}~u^{\prime}
and, by substitution property of precision,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B[u/x]\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B^{\prime}[u^{\prime}/x]
.

Inference—Other diagonal cases.

All other cases are similar to those: combining the induction hypothesis directly leads to the desired result, handling the binders in a similar way to that of products when needed.

Checking.

For
9Type-directed elaboration from

GCIC

to

CastCIC

, we have that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S
, with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S\sim\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
. By induction hypothesis,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}
with
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}
. But we also have as an hypothesis that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
. By monotonicity of consistency, we conclude that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}\sim\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
, and thus
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleleft}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T^{\prime}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t^{\prime}}
. A use of
10Structural precision in

CastCIC

then ensures that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S}\rangle\,{t}\sqsubseteq_{\alpha}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{T^{\prime}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t^{\prime}}
, as desired.

Constrained inference—
9Type-directed elaboration from

GCIC

to

CastCIC

rule.

We are in the situation where
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A.B
. By induction hypothesis,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}
with

17
, we get that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}S^{\prime}\operatorname{{\leadsto}^{\ast}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\Pi}x:A^{\prime}.B^{\prime}

to

CastCIC

we get

is larger than the universe levels of both

, we get that

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.88,0}\tilde{t}^{\prime}\operatorname{\rightsquigarrow}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{\operatorname{germ}_{i}\,\Pi}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{S^{\prime}}\rangle\,{t^{\prime}}\operatorname{\vbox{\hbox{${\scriptscriptstyle\blacktriangleright}$}}_{\Pi}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{germ}_{i}\,\Pi

is enough to conclude.

Constrained inference—Other rules.

All other cases are similar to the previous ones, albeit with a simpler handling of universe levels (since
c_{\Pi}
does not appear).

∎

Appendix C
Connecting the discrete and monotone models

Comparing the discrete and the monotone translations, we can see that they
coincide on ground types such as
\mathbb{N}
.
On functions over ground types, for instance
\mathbb{N}{\to}\mathbb{N}
, the
monotone interpretation is more conservative:
any monotone function
f:\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{N}{\to}\mathbb{N}}\!\mid\!\}
induces a function

\tilde{f}:\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{N}{\to}\mathbb{N}}\rrbracket{}
by forgetting the monotonicity, but not all
functions from
\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{N}{\to}\mathbb{N}}\rrbracket{}
are monotone
25
25

25

For instance the
function swapping
\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\ddot{\mathbb{N}}{}}
and
{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\ddot{\mathbb{N}}{}}
is not monotone.
.

Translation of contexts

\displaystyle\Lbag{}{\cdot}\Rbag{}\quad

\displaystyle:=\quad{\cdot}

\displaystyle\Lbag{}{\Gamma,x:A}\Rbag{}\quad

Logical relation on terms and types

\begin{array}[t]{lcl}\Lbag{}{A}\Rbag&:=&\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,\lbag{}{A}\!\rbag\\[6.99997pt]

Inductive-recursive relational universe

Decoding function
\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}:\square\!\!\!\!\square_{\text{rel}}\,A\,A^{\prime}{\to}\mathrm{El}\,A{\to}\mathrm{El}\,A^{\prime}{\to}\square{}

\displaystyle\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,\widehat{\square\!\!\!\!\square}_{\text{rel},j}~A~A^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\square\!\!\!\!\square_{\text{rel},j}~A~A^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\text{rel}}~n~m

\displaystyle\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,\maltese_{\text{rel}}~\mathtt{()}~\mathtt{()}

\displaystyle:=\mathtt{unit}

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{\Pi}(a:\mathrm{El}\,A)(a^{\prime}:\mathrm{El}\,A^{\prime})(a_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,A_{\text{rel}}~a~a^{\prime}).

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,(B_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}})~(f\,a)~(f^{\prime}\,a^{\prime})

.
Logical relation between the discrete and monotone models

Extending the sketched correspondence at higher types, we obtain a (binary)
logical relation
\lbag{}{-}\!\rbag{}
between terms of the discrete and monotone
translations described in
Fig.

20
, that forgets the
monotonicity information on ground types.
More precisely we define for each types
A
in the source a relation

\Lbag{}{A}\Rbag{}:\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\rrbracket{}\to\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\!\mid\!\}\to\square{}
and for each term
t:A
a
witness
\lbag{}{t}\!\rbag{}:\Lbag{}{A}\Rbag{}~[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}]~\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}
.

The logical relation employs a an inductively defined relation
\square\!\!\!\!\square_{\text{rel},i}

between
\square\!\!\!\!\square_{i}^{\text{dis}}:=\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}}\rrbracket{}
and
\square\!\!\!\!\square_{i}^{\text{mon}}:=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}}\!\mid\!\}

whose constructors are relational codes relating codes of discrete and monotone
types.
These relational codes are then decoded to relations between the corresponding
decoded types thanks to
{\mathrm{El}{}}_{\text{rel}}
.
The main difficult case in establishing the logical relation lie in relating
the
\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}
s, since that’s the main point of divergence of the two models.

Lemma 1 (Basis lemma).

(1)

There exists a term
\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}_{\text{rel}}:\Lbag{}{\operatorname{\Pi}(A\,B:\square\!\!\!\!\square).A\to B}\Rbag{}~[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]~\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}
.

(2)

More generally, if
\Gamma\vdash_{\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}t:A
then
\Lbag{}{\Gamma}\Rbag{}\vdash_{\mathrm{IR}}\lbag{}{t}\!\rbag{}:\Lbag{}{A}\Rbag{}~[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}]~\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}
.

In particular
\mathsf{CastCIC}
terms of ground types behave similarly in both models.

Expanding the type of
\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}_{\text{rel}}
, we need to provide a
term

c_{\text{rel}}=\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}_{\text{rel}}\,A\,A^{\prime}\,A_{\text{rel}}\,B\,B^{\prime}\,B_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,B_{\text{rel}}~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]\,A\,B\,a)~(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,A^{\prime}\,B^{\prime}\,a^{\prime})

where

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square{}_{i}}\rrbracket{},

\displaystyle A^{\prime}

\displaystyle:\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square{}_{i}}\!\mid\!\},

\displaystyle A_{\text{rel}}

\displaystyle:\square\!\!\!\!\square_{\text{rel}}\,A\,A^{\prime},

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:\llbracket{}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square{}_{i}}\rrbracket{},

\displaystyle B^{\prime}

\displaystyle:\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square{}_{i}}\!\mid\!\},

\displaystyle B_{\text{rel}}

\displaystyle:\square\!\!\!\!\square_{\text{rel}}\,B\,B^{\prime},

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle:\mathrm{El}\,A,

\displaystyle a^{\prime}

\displaystyle:\mathrm{El}\,A^{\prime},

\displaystyle a_{\text{rel}}

\displaystyle:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,A_{\text{rel}}~a~a^{\prime}

We proceed by induction on
A_{\text{rel}},B_{\text{rel}}
, following the
defining cases for
[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]
(see
Fig.

14
).

Case
A_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}
and

B_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}\,B^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,B^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}
:

we pose
{A^{\prime}=\widehat{\Pi}\,A^{\prime\text{d}}\,A^{\prime\text{c}}}
and
{B^{\prime}=\widehat{\Pi}\,B^{\prime\text{d}}\,B^{\prime\text{c}}}

(by definition of
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}
)

\displaystyle\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~A^{\prime}~B^{\prime}~f^{\prime}

\displaystyle=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{B^{\prime}}(\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime}}f^{\prime})

(by decomposition of
\widehat{\Pi}\sqsubseteq\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}
)

\displaystyle=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{B^{\prime}}\circ\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime}}(f)

(by section-retraction identity)

\displaystyle=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{B^{\prime}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime}}(f)

(by def. of ep-pair on
\Pi
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\lambda}(b^{\prime}:\mathrm{El}\,A^{\prime\text{d}}).~\text{let}\,a^{\prime}=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{B^{\prime\text{d}}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime\text{d}}}(b)\,\text{in}\,

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{B^{\prime\text{c}}\,b^{\prime}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime\text{c}}\,a^{\prime}}(f\,a^{\prime})

(by definition of
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\lambda}(b^{\prime}:\mathrm{El}\,A^{\prime\text{d}}).~\text{let}\,a^{\prime}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~B^{\prime\text{d}}~A^{\prime\text{d}}~b^{\prime}\,\text{in}\,

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,(A^{\prime\text{c}}\,a^{\prime})~(B^{\prime\text{c}}\,b^{\prime})~(f\,a^{\prime})

For any
b:\mathrm{El}\,B^{\text{d}}
and
b^{\prime}:\mathrm{El}\,B^{\prime\text{d}}
,
b_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,B^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,b\,b^{\prime}
, we have by inductive hypothesis

a_{\text{rel}}:=\lbag{}{\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\!\rbag{}\,B^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,b_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}~{A_{\text{rel}}}~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]\,B^{\text{d}}\,A^{\text{d}}\,b)~(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,B^{\prime\text{d}}\,A^{\prime\text{d}}\,b^{\prime})

so that, posing
a=[{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]\,B^{\text{d}}\,A^{\text{d}}\,b
and
a^{\prime}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,B^{\prime\text{d}}\,A^{\prime\text{d}}\,b^{\prime}
,

f_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}~(A^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}})~(f\,a)~(f^{\prime}\,a^{\prime})

and by another application of the inductive hypothesis

\lbag{}{\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\!\rbag{}~(B^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}\,b\,b^{\prime}\,b_{\text{rel}})~(A^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}})~(f_{\text{rel}}\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{\text{rel}}):\Lbag{}{B^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}\,b\,b^{\prime}\,b_{\text{rel}}}\Rbag{}~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]~A~B~f~a)~(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~A^{\prime}~B^{\prime}~f^{\prime}~a^{\prime})

Packing these together, we obtain a term

\lbag{}{\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\!\rbag{}~A_{\text{rel}}~B_{\text{rel}}~f_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}~(\widehat{\Pi}~B^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}~B^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}})~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]~A~B~f)~(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~A^{\prime}~B^{\prime}~f^{\prime}).

Case
A_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}
and

B_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}}
:

By definition of the logical relation at
\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}}
, we need to build a
witness of type

\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}~(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}^{c_{\Pi}(i)}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}^{c_{\Pi}(i)})~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]~A~(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}})~f)~(\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~A^{\prime}~\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}~f^{\prime}))

We compute that

\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,A^{\prime}\,\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\,f^{\prime})=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}\circ\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime}}f^{\prime}=~\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}\circ\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{A^{\prime}}f^{\prime}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,A^{\prime}\,(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}})\,f^{\prime}

So the result holds by induction hypothesis.

Other cases with

A_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,A^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}
:

It is enough to show that
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,A^{\prime}\,B^{\prime}\,f^{\prime}=\maltese_{B^{\prime}}
when
B^{\prime}=\maltese
(trivial) or
\mathrm{head}\,\,B^{\prime}\neq\texttt{pi}
. The latter case holds because

\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\operatorname{germ}\,c}\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\operatorname{germ}\,c^{\prime}}x=\maltese_{\mathrm{El}_{\operatorname{Head}}\,c}
whenever
c\neq c^{\prime}
and downcasts preserve
\maltese
.

Case
A_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}}
,

B_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}\,B^{\text{d}}_{\text{rel}}\,B^{\text{c}}_{\text{rel}}
and

a=(\texttt{pi};f)
:

By hypothesis,
a_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}~(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}})~f~(\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}a^{\prime})
and

\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}~B^{\prime}~a^{\prime}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}~(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}})~B^{\prime}~(\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}a^{\prime})
so by induction hypothesis

\lbag{}{\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\!\rbag{}~(\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}}\to_{\text{rel}}\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}})~B_{\text{rel}}~f~(\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\to\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}a^{\prime})~a_{\text{rel}}:\mathrm{El}_{\text{rel}}\,B_{\text{rel}}~([{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}]\,\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\,B\,(\texttt{pi};f))~(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{cast}}}\}\,\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}\,B^{\prime}\,a^{\prime})

The others cases with
A_{\text{rel}}=\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}_{\text{rel}}
proceed in a
similarly fashion.
All cases with
A_{\text{rel}}=\maltese_{\text{rel}}{}
are immediate since

\maltese^{\text{dis}}
and
\maltese^{\text{mon}}
are related at any related types.
Finally, the cases with
A_{\text{rel}}{}=\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\text{rel}}
follow the same pattern
as for
\widehat{\Pi}_{\text{rel}}
.
∎

Appendix D
Diverging terms denote as errors in
\omega
-cpos

In this section we define a logical relation between
\mathsf{CastCIC}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathsf{CIC^{IR}_{QIT}}
and
prove a fundamental lemma, obtaining
Lemma

9
as a corollary.
The logical relation is presented in

Figs.

21
,
22
and
23

and relates types
A
in
\mathsf{CastCIC}^{\mathcal{G}}
with sub-
\omega
-cpos of
\ddot{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}
,
following the description of
\mathrm{El}
in that model.
A type
A
related to an
\omega
-cpo
A^{\prime}
by the logical relation, noted
A\sim A^{\prime}
, induces a relation between terms of type
A
and elements of
A^{\prime}
.
We use variables with
\varepsilon
subscript to name proof witnesses of
relatedness between two objects, for instance
A_{\varepsilon}:A\sim A^{\prime}
, and
bold variables such as
\mathbb{\Gamma},\mathbb{\Delta}
for the corresponding double contexts
consisting of variable bindings
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}
.
The projections
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}
and
\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}
are then respectively contexts
in
\mathsf{CastCIC}^{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathsf{CIC^{IR}_{QIT}}
.

The logical relation uses weak head reduction to characterize divergence.
We note
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u
when a
\mathsf{CastCIC}
term
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
reduces to a
weak head normal form, that is a term
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u
such that
\operatorname{canonical}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u
hold (see
Fig.

7
), using
only weak head reduction steps.
We note
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}
when weak head reduction paths from
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
never reach a
weak head normal form, that is
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
is unsolvable.

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash
logical relation between
\mathsf{CastCIC}^{\mathcal{N}}
contexts and

\omega
-cpos.

\mpr@inferrule\square{}{\cdot\Vdash}\mpr@inferrule\square{}{}{\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\\
A_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\sim A^{\prime}}{\mathbb{\Gamma},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}\Vdash}{\begin{array}[]{l@{~=~}l}\cdot_{1}&\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\cdot\\
(\mathbb{\Gamma},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon})_{1}&\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1},a:A\end{array}}

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A
and

\omega
-cpos

.

We first state a lemma making explicit how divergence is accounted for by the
logical relation.

Lemma 1 (Diverging terms relate to errors).

(1)

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t:\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
,
T_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim T^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}
then
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T^{\prime}}:T_{\varepsilon}
.

(2)

Conversely, if
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t:\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
,
T_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim T^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim t^{\prime}:T_{\varepsilon}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}
then
t^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T^{\prime}}
.

In the two parts of the lemma, we proceed by induction on
T_{\varepsilon}
.
For the first part, the cases
T_{\varepsilon}=\square_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{N}_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon}
and

{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\varepsilon}
are immediate because in each case a rule apply for diverging terms.
If
T_{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\varepsilon}
, then

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\mathtt{()}:T_{\varepsilon}
which is enough because
\mathtt{()}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\mathtt{unit}}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\mathrm{El}\,\widehat{\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}}}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\mathrm{El}\,T^{\prime}}
.
Finally, if
T_{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{\Pi}_{\varepsilon}\,A_{\varepsilon}\,B_{\varepsilon}
, then
for any
\rho:\mathbb{\Delta}\subset\mathbb{\Gamma}
and
a_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}\,\rho

we have that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t[\rho_{1}]~a:\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B[\rho_{1},a]
,

B_{\varepsilon}\,\rho\,a_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B[\rho_{1},a]\sim B^{\prime}[\rho_{2},a^{\prime}]
and

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t~a\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}
, so by induction hypothesis

\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t[\rho_{1}]~a\sim\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B^{\prime}[\rho_{2},a^{\prime}]}:B_{\varepsilon}\,\rho\,a_{\varepsilon}
,
hence
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\widehat{\Pi}\,A^{\prime}\,B^{\prime}}:T_{\varepsilon}
.

We now turn to the second part of the lemma.
When
T_{\varepsilon}=\square_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{N}_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon},{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\varepsilon}
and

\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\varepsilon}
, there is exactly one rule that apply to relate to a term without
weak head normal form
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
so that necessarily
t^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T^{\prime}}
.
When
T_{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{\Pi}_{\varepsilon}\,A_{\varepsilon}\,B_{\varepsilon}
, any
\rho:\mathbb{\Delta}\subset\mathbb{\Gamma}
and
a_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}\,\rho
we have that

B_{\varepsilon}\,\rho\,a_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B[\rho_{1},a]\sim B^{\prime}[\rho_{2},a^{\prime}]
,

\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t[\rho_{1}]~a\sim t^{\prime}[\rho_{2}]~a^{\prime}:B_{\varepsilon}\,\rho\,a_{\varepsilon}
and

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t~a\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}
, so by induction hypothesis
t^{\prime}\,a^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B^{\prime}[\rho_{2},a^{\prime}]}
.
Taking
\rho
to be the weakening
\mathbb{\Gamma},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathbb{\Gamma}
, we have by function extensionality that
t^{\prime}=\lambda(a^{\prime}:A^{\prime}).\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B^{\prime}}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\widehat{\Pi}\,A^{\prime}\,B^{\prime}}
.
∎

Lemma 2 (Fundamental lemma).

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash
then there exists
\mathbb{\Gamma}
such that

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}=\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
and
\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma}\!\mid\!\}
;

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
there
exists a derivation
T_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\!\mid\!\}

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
then there exists
a derivation
t_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}:T_{\varepsilon}

•

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
,

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\equiv\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
then
\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\!\mid\!\}=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}}\!\mid\!\}
and
T_{\varepsilon}=T^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\!\mid\!\}
.

Since the translation
\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}-}\!\mid\!\}
underly a model of
\mathsf{CastCIC}^{\mathcal{N}}
, it sends
convertible types
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T,\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}
in the source to provably equal types
in the target
\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\!\mid\!\}=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T^{\prime}}\!\mid\!\}
, proving the last claim.

The three other claims are proved by mutual induction on the input derivation,
assuming an undirected variant of the rules
in
Figs.

3
and
1
, which is possible
by
(
Lennon-Bertrand 2021
)
.
Concretely, this modification means that we assume additional well-formedness
premises in the derivations,
e.g.,
for contexts and types, and do not show that
input well-formedness is preserved.
Moreover the induction hypothesis needs to be strenghened to quantify over an
arbitrary context
\mathbb{\Delta}
with a substition
\sigma:\mathbb{\Delta}\to\mathbb{\Gamma}
whose
components are related according to the logical relation.

For contexts, if the derivation ends with a rule
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
, it is enough take
\mathbb{\Gamma}=\cdot
.
If it ends with
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
, then by induction hypothesis
there exists
\mathbb{\Gamma}
and
A_{\varepsilon}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}=\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
,
\mathbb{\Gamma}_{2}=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma}\!\mid\!\}
,
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash
and

A_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\sim\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\!\mid\!\}
, so taking
\mathbb{\Gamma},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}
suffices.

For
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
by induction hypothesis
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash
with

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}=\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma
. Moreover,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i+1}
and
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}}\}=\widehat{\square\!\!\!\!\square}_{i}
so

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}}\}:\square_{\varepsilon}\,(i+1)
and

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i}}\}
.
The rules
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
(for
\mathbb{N},\mathbb{B}
) and

1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
(for
0,\texttt{suc},\mathtt{true},\mathtt{false}
),
introducing types and terms that are already
in weak head normal form follow the same pattern as
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
.
In the case of the rules
3

CastCIC

: Bidirectional typing (extending

CIC

,
replacing )
and

1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
, the context needs to be extended and we need to
take advantage of the full induction hypothesis strenghened under arbitrary
reducible substitutions.

Dually, the rule
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
is immediate by induction
hypothesis and the definition the logical relation at function types.
A bit more work is needed for the rule
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
for

\mathtt{ind}_{\mathbb{B}}
and
\mathtt{ind}_{\mathbb{N}}
, doing a case analysis on the proof of
relatedness of their main argument.
If the main argument diverges, then the applied eliminator diverges too so it
is related to
\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}
which is its translation because eliminators send errors
at an inductive type to errors at the adequate type in
\omega
-cpos.
Otherwise the main argument weak head reduces to a normal form and we can
conclude by induction hypothesis and closure by anti-reduction.

For the variable case, rule
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
, we can show by
induction on the proof of relatedness of its type that it is related to its

\eta
-expansion at
\Pi
types an to itself at any other type using the rules
for neutrals.
We conclude by extensionality of the
\omega
-cpo model.

Conversion rules
1

: Bidirectional typing
,

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
,
1

CIC

: Bidirectional typing
and

1

: Bidirectional typing
satisfy the fundamental lemma because
convertible types induce the same relation on their term.

For
3

CastCIC

: Bidirectional typing (extending

CIC

,
replacing )
, we have by induction hypothesis that

T_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\}:\square_{\varepsilon}
.
By case analysis,
T_{\varepsilon}
is necessarily one of
\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\varepsilon},\operatorname{\Pi}_{\varepsilon},{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{N}_{\varepsilon}
or
\square_{\varepsilon}
.
If
T_{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\varepsilon}\,\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
then

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T}\sim\mathtt{()}:\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\varepsilon}\,\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
since
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{\Gamma}_{1}\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T}\operatorname{\triangleright}{T}
, and we can conclude using extensionality of
\mathtt{unit}=\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\!\mid\!\}
, that is
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T}}\}=\mathtt{()}
.
If
T_{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{\Pi}_{\varepsilon}\,A_{\varepsilon}\,B_{\varepsilon}
, then for any

\rho:\mathbb{\Delta}\subset\mathbb{\Gamma}
and
a_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}a\sim a^{\prime}:A_{\varepsilon}\,\rho
we have that

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T[\rho_{1}]\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Pi(a:A)B
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{T}~a\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B[a]}
, so we conclude this case by induction hypothesis

\mathbb{\Delta}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B[a]}\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B[a]}}\}:B_{\varepsilon}\,a_{\varepsilon}
,
closure by anti-reduction and the fact that
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{B[a]}}\}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B[a]}\}}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\}[a^{\prime}]}
.

In all the other cases
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
weak head reduces to a type in weak head
normal form
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\square},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\square_{i},\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{N}
or

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathbb{B}
, and a corresponding rule is present in the logical relation to
conclude directly.
A similar proof apply for the rule
3

CastCIC

: Bidirectional typing (extending

CIC

,
replacing )
.

Finally, for the rule
3

CastCIC

: Bidirectional typing (extending

CIC

,
replacing )
with conclusion

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B
, we have by
induction hypothesis we have that
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash,{A_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A\sim\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\!\mid\!\}},B_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B\sim\{\!\mid\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\!\mid\!\}
and

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}:A_{\varepsilon}
.
By analysing all possible weak head reduction paths from

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}
, either:

(a):

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u

such that
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u}\}:B_{\varepsilon}
using
inversions on
A_{\varepsilon},B_{\varepsilon}
and
t_{\varepsilon}
, or

(b):

one of

\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A
,
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B
or
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t
never reduces to a weak head normal form.

In case
(a)
, we conclude that

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}}\}:B_{\varepsilon}

by closure under anti-reduction and using the fact that

\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}}\}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}u}\}
(because
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}-}\}
maps
convertible terms to equal terms in the model).
In case
(b)
, we have that

\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}\sim\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\}}:B_{\varepsilon}
by
the first part of
Lemma

1
and the second part of that lemma
ensures that one of
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\}
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\}
or

\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}
is an error at the adequate type so that

\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\langle{B}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{A}\rangle\,{t}}\}=\downarrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\}}\uparrow^{\widehat{{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}A}\}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}B}\}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.

If
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Gamma\vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\operatorname{\triangleright}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T
and
\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\not\twoheadrightarrow_{\mathrm{wh}}

then
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\}}
.

By the fundamental lemma,
\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}:T_{\varepsilon}

with
T_{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{\Gamma}\Vdash\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T\sim\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\}:\square_{\varepsilon}

and by the second part of
Lemma

1
,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}t}\}=\operatorname{\mathtt{err}}_{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}T}\}}
.
∎

Appendix E
A Direct Presentation of Vectors

Figure 24.
Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.

Vectors have two new normal forms, corresponding to cast of

nil
and
cons
to
\mathtt{vec}~A~{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\mathbb{N}}
. The difference
with the treatment of the universe is that the corresponding term,
for instance
\langle{\mathtt{vec}~A~{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\mathbb{N}}}{\ \Leftarrow\ }{\mathtt{vec}~A~n}\rangle\,{t}
for the
case of
nil
, can not be considered as canonical form because
they involve a non-linear occurrence of
A
.
To remedy to this issue, we add two new canonical forms (
\mathtt{nil}_{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}~A
and

\mathtt{cons}_{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}~A~a~n~v
) to vectors
with introduction typing rules defined in
Fig.

24
.

Regarding cast on vectors, it does not only compute in the argument of
the cast as it is the case for inductive types without indices, but it
also computes on the indices.
That is, a cast on vectors is neutral when either one of the indices
is neutral or the argument is neutral (see
Fig.

24
).
Other kind of neutral can be derived from the one of inductive types
without indices and are omitted here.

Similarly, we do not detail the other typing rules for vectors as they
are similar to the one for inductive types wihtout indices, and focus
on explaining the new reduction rules, presented also in

Fig.

24
.

The two first reduction rules
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
and

24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
are standard reduction rules in
\mathsf{CIC}
for the
recursor
vect_rect
on vectors.
The rules
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
and
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
are
the standard rules dealing with exceptions.
Additionally, there are two computation rules for the eliminator on
the two new constructors
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
and

24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
which basically consist in the underlying
non-exceptional constructor to the eliminator and cast the result back
to
P~{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}_{\mathbb{N}}
.
This rule somehow transfers the cast on vectors to a cast on
the returned type of the predicate.

Finally, there are rules to conduct casts between vectors
in canonical forms.
The last three rules (
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
,
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
and

24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
) are simply propogation of errors.
Then, there remains 12 rules, 3 by constructors of vectors. We just
explain the one on
cons
.
Rule
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
applies when both indices of the form
S

of somthing and progates the cast of the arguments, as does the
standard rule for casting a constructor.
Rule
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
detects that the indices do not match and
raise an error.
Finally, Rule
24Canonical forms and reduction rule for vectors.
propagates the cast on the arguments,
but this time applied to
\mathtt{cons}_{\operatorname{\boldsymbol{?}}}
, thus converting precise
information to a less precise information.
